# Bugs found by the test pipeline

Found while writing specs. Not yet ticketed. Tests for these are written to the
intended behavior and marked `xfail(strict=True, reason="BUG-NNN")`.

| ID | Pattern | Module | Title | Today | Intended | Spec | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BUG-001 | `capped-uniqueness-scan` | `app/managers/department_manager.py` | Duplicate-name check only scans the first 100 departments | Uniqueness check loads at most 100 unordered rows (deleted included); a live duplicate outside them is missed and a duplicate is created | Duplicate detection covers all live departments of the workspace | [spec Q1](../../tests/unit/managers/department_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-002 | `unnormalised-uniqueness-compare` | `app/managers/department_manager.py` | Duplicate-name check is case- and whitespace-sensitive | Names compared with exact `==`; "Sales", "sales", "Sales " all coexist | Compare case-insensitively after trimming leading/trailing whitespace | [spec Q2](../../tests/unit/managers/department_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-003 | `python-only-uniqueness` | `app/managers/department_manager.py` | Department name uniqueness not enforced by the database | No unique constraint on `(workspace_id, name)`; concurrent creates/renames can both succeed | Partial unique index on live rows `(workspace_id, normalised name)`; integrity violation surfaces as 409 | [spec Q3](../../tests/unit/managers/department_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-004 | `blank-name-accepted` | `app/managers/department_manager.py` (fix in `app/schemas/department.py`) | Blank / whitespace-only department names accepted | Create accepts `""` and `"   "`; update with `""` skips duplicate check and blanks the name | `DepartmentCreate`/`DepartmentUpdate` strip whitespace and require min length 1 (Pydantic `ValidationError`, 422 at the API), nothing written; names stored trimmed | [spec Q4, Q11](../../tests/unit/managers/department_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-005 | `get-returns-soft-deleted` | `app/managers/department_manager.py` (+ `DepartmentService.get_department` and `GET /departments/{id}/` pass `include_deleted`, HR-4: [service spec](../../tests/unit/services/department_service.spec.md)) | `get_department` returns soft-deleted departments | Deleted department returned with 200 | Deleted → 404 by default; new `include_deleted=True` parameter returns it | [spec Q5](../../tests/unit/managers/department_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-006 | `paginate-before-filter` | `app/managers/department_manager.py` | Department list drops deleted rows after paging | `skip`/`limit` applied to all rows incl. deleted, then deleted removed in Python; pages short or empty | Exclude deleted before paging; `skip`/`limit` count only returnable departments | [spec Q6](../../tests/unit/managers/department_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-007 | `paginate-before-filter` | `app/managers/department_manager.py` | Department name search only searches the current page | Search substring applied in Python to the loaded page only | Search applied across the whole workspace before paging; once in SQL, search text escaped so `%` and `_` match literally (HR-8) | [spec Q7, HR-8 line](../../tests/unit/managers/department_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-008 | `unordered-pagination` | `app/managers/department_manager.py` | Department list has no sort order | No `ORDER BY`; paging can repeat or skip departments | Order by name case-insensitive ascending, `id` ascending as tie-breaker, applied before paging | [spec Q8, Q12](../../tests/unit/managers/department_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-009 | `audit-stamp-on-noop` | `app/managers/department_manager.py` | `updated_by` stamped on no-op updates | Every successful update sets `updated_by`, even `{}`, `{"name": null}` or unchanged name | Set `updated_by` only when a field actually changes | [spec Q9](../../tests/unit/managers/department_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-010 | `flag-unreachable-from-api` | `app/managers/department_manager.py` (fix in `DepartmentService` + `GET /departments/`) | List `include_deleted` flag unreachable from the API | Service and endpoint never pass `include_deleted`; deleted departments cannot be listed | Expose `include_deleted` on the list API (service + endpoint), consistent with get's `include_deleted` (BUG-005) | [spec Q10](../../tests/unit/managers/department_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-011 | `flag-unreachable-from-api` | `app/dao/item.py` (fix in `GET /items/similar/`) | Similar-name `exclude_item_id` unreachable from the API | Endpoint never passes `exclude_item_id`; an item being renamed is reported as a duplicate of itself | `GET /items/similar/` accepts `exclude_item_id` and passes it through to the service | [spec Q3](../../tests/unit/dao/item.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-012 | `unicode-digit-int-parse` | `app/dao/item.py` | Catalog search with non-ASCII digits (e.g. `²`) crashes | `str.isdigit()` accepts any Unicode digit; `int("²")` raises `ValueError` → 500; `"١٢"` matches id 12 | Only ASCII `0-9` search text also matches item id; any other digit characters are searched as text only and never raise | [spec Q5](../../tests/unit/dao/item.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-013 | `unescaped-like-wildcards` | `app/dao/item.py` | `%` and `_` in catalog search act as wildcards | Search text inserted into `ILIKE '%…%'` unescaped; `_` or `%` alone matches every item | `%`, `_` and (HR-8) `\` matched literally (escaped) in all search alternatives, incl. tag names | [spec Q6, HR-8 violation](../../tests/unit/dao/item.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-014 | `join-ignores-soft-deleted` | `app/dao/item.py` | Filtering by a deleted tag still returns items | Tag filter ignores `ItemTag.is_active`; items assigned to a deleted tag match | Assignments to deleted tags (`is_active = False`) do not match the tag filter | [spec Q7](../../tests/unit/dao/item.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-015 | `join-ignores-soft-deleted` | `app/dao/item.py` | Catalog search matches names of deleted tags | Tag-name search ignores `ItemTag.is_active` | Tag-name search considers only active tags | [spec Q8](../../tests/unit/dao/item.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-016 | `missing-limit-lower-bound` | `app/dao/item.py` (fix in `GET /items/`) | Item list accepts `limit` ≤ 0 | No minimum on `limit`: `0` → empty page with `has_more: true` forever; `-1` → Postgres error → 500 | `GET /items/` requires `limit >= 1` (422 otherwise) | [spec Q9](../../tests/unit/dao/item.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-017 | `unnormalised-value-grouping` | `app/dao/item.py` (storage trim in item create/update schema/manager) | Unit list and unit filter treat each spelling separately | Units stored as typed; distinct list and filter are exact and case-sensitive ("kg", "Kg", "kg " separate) | Units stored trimmed; unit filter value trimmed and compared case-insensitively (as BUG-002/BUG-004); unit filter and unit list compare/group on the trimmed, lowercased stored unit (legacy `"kg "` merges with `"kg"`; whitespace-only never listed); unit list has one entry per unit, shown in lowercase | [spec Q10, Q12, Q14](../../tests/unit/dao/item.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-018 | `unordered-pagination` | `app/dao/item.py` | Similar-name suggestions with tied scores in no fixed order | `ORDER BY score DESC` only; tied rows surviving `LIMIT` vary between calls | Ties broken by name case-insensitive ascending, then `id` ascending, before `LIMIT` | [spec Q11](../../tests/unit/dao/item.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-019 | `hr-13-unrecorded-external-refusal` | `app/managers/payment_transaction_manager.py` (+ model column, `PaymentTransactionService`) | Refused checkout start leaves no record and returns 502 | Gateway status ≠ `SUCCESS` → 502, no transaction row, no event, `failed_reason` never filled | HR-13: save transaction with status `INIT_FAILED` and `failed_reason` (fallback `unknown error`), log `initiation_failed` event, return normally with `status`/`failed_reason` in the response | [spec Q1](../../tests/unit/managers/payment_transaction_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-020 | `metadata-not-forwarded` | `app/managers/payment_transaction_manager.py` (+ `InitSessionRequest`) | `value_a`..`value_d` never sent to the gateway | Stored on our row only; gateway never receives or echoes them | `value_a`..`value_d` included in the gateway session request | [spec Q2](../../tests/unit/managers/payment_transaction_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-021 | `unbounded-decimal-input` | `app/managers/payment_transaction_manager.py` (fix in `app/schemas/payment_transaction.py`) | Amounts with > 2 decimals or ≥ 10^13 accepted | `10.005` sent unrounded but stored `10.01` → later "Amount mismatch"; ≥ 10^13 fails at flush after the gateway session exists | `InitiatePaymentRequest` rejects > 2 decimals or > 13 integer digits (422) before the gateway is called | [spec Q3](../../tests/unit/managers/payment_transaction_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-022 | `unvalidated-code-input` | `app/managers/payment_transaction_manager.py` (fix in `app/schemas/payment_transaction.py`) | Currency code not validated or normalised | Any 3-char string stored as typed; `bdt` later fails "Currency mismatch" against gateway `BDT` | Currency upper-cased; only supported codes (`BDT`, `USD`) accepted, anything else 422 | [spec Q4](../../tests/unit/managers/payment_transaction_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-023 | `hr-11-unverified-input-applied` | `app/managers/payment_transaction_manager.py` | Unknown or foreign `val_id` permanently fails the transaction | Gateway `INVALID_TRANSACTION` or a `val_id` resolving to another `tran_id` → `VALIDATED_FAILED`; genuine result later ignored | HR-11: logged as `validation_rejected` event and ignored; transaction unchanged, later genuine result still finalizes it | [spec Q5](../../tests/unit/managers/payment_transaction_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-024 | `hr-12-missing-confirmation-passes` | `app/managers/payment_transaction_manager.py` | Missing amount/currency in gateway answer counts as a match | Amount `None` or currency `None`/`""` skips the check; can become `VALIDATED_SUCCESS` | HR-12: VALID answer missing amount or currency → `RISK_HOLD` (a definite mismatch still → `VALIDATED_FAILED`) | [spec Q6](../../tests/unit/managers/payment_transaction_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-025 | `channel-dependent-outcome` | `app/managers/payment_transaction_manager.py` | Cancelled payment status depends on which channel arrives first | Cancel redirect → `CANCELLED`; gateway-validated `CANCELLED` (IPN) → `VALIDATED_FAILED` | Gateway-reported `CANCELLED` → status `CANCELLED` on every channel (callback and sweep) | [spec Q7](../../tests/unit/managers/payment_transaction_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-026 | `hr-11-unverified-signal-final` | `app/managers/payment_transaction_manager.py` | Unverified redirect outcome blocks later verified result | Fail/cancel redirect (or success without `val_id`) sets a final status; later verified IPN ignored as duplicate | HR-11: redirect outcomes recorded as unverified; a later gateway-verified result replaces them (requires new column `outcome_verified` BOOLEAN NOT NULL default `false`; unset = unverified, per spec Q16) | [spec Q8, Q16](../../tests/unit/managers/payment_transaction_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-027 | `hr-11-unverified-signal-final` | `app/managers/payment_transaction_manager.py` | Verified result after `EXPIRED` is ignored | Sweep's `EXPIRED` is final; a later valid IPN is logged as duplicate | HR-11: a gateway-verified result arriving after `EXPIRED` replaces it | [spec Q9](../../tests/unit/managers/payment_transaction_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-028 | `undocumented-event-types` | `app/managers/payment_transaction_manager.py` | Event type names differ from the documented list | `redirect_success_received`/`redirect_fail_received`, `cancelled`; no-`val_id` redirects logged as `validated_failed` | Documented names: `redirect_success`, `redirect_fail`, `redirect_cancel`, `ipn_received`; unverified redirect outcomes never logged as `validated_failed` (new-type names pending spec Q15) | [spec Q10](../../tests/unit/managers/payment_transaction_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-029 | `hr-10-unlocked-state-transition` | `app/managers/payment_transaction_manager.py` | Risk-hold resolution does not lock the row | Row read without lock; two concurrent resolves can both succeed | HR-10: row read `FOR UPDATE`; the second resolve sees the new status and gets 400 | [spec Q11](../../tests/unit/managers/payment_transaction_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-030 | `lossy-status-mapping` | `app/managers/payment_transaction_manager.py` | Sweep labels gateway failures/cancellations as `EXPIRED` | Any gateway answer other than `VALID`/`VALIDATED` → `EXPIRED` | `VALID`/`VALIDATED` validated as usual, `FAILED` → `VALIDATED_FAILED`, `CANCELLED` → `CANCELLED`; only `UNATTEMPTED`/`EXPIRED`/no result (and other) → `EXPIRED` | [spec Q12](../../tests/unit/managers/payment_transaction_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-031 | `sweep-lookup-requires-callback-data` | `app/managers/payment_transaction_manager.py` (fix mainly in `app/integrations/sslcommerz/mock_client.py`) | Sweep can never recover a paid transaction with the mock gateway | Sweep passes the always-`None` stored `val_id` as hint; mock answers `UNATTEMPTED` → `EXPIRED` | Sweep looks up by `tran_id` alone and the mock supports that lookup; a paid transaction with lost callbacks is recovered | [spec Q13](../../tests/unit/managers/payment_transaction_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-032 | `hr-14-batch-aborts-on-row-error` | `app/managers/payment_transaction_manager.py` (+ `scripts/reconcile_payments.py`) | One gateway error aborts the whole sweep | Exception propagates; later candidates skipped and the script rolls back all results | HR-14: failing candidate stays `INITIATED` with a `reconciliation_error` event and is skipped; others completed and kept (per-candidate savepoint) | [spec Q14](../../tests/unit/managers/payment_transaction_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-033 | `hr-6-non-default-list-order` | `app/dao/item.py` | Catalog item list ordered by `id` instead of name | `list_active_items_filtered` orders by `id` ascending only | HR-6: name case-insensitive ascending, then `id` ascending, applied before `skip`/`limit` | [spec HR-6 violation](../../tests/unit/dao/item.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-034 | `float-precision-threshold` | `app/dao/item.py` | Similarity score of exactly 0.35 excluded | `real` score (7/20 = 0.3499999940…) compared against 0.35 in double precision → below threshold | Threshold compared in the score's own `real` precision; a score of exactly 0.35 is included | [spec Q13](../../tests/unit/dao/item.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-035 | `missing-workspace-scope` | `app/services/status_service.py`, `app/api/v1/endpoints/statuses.py` | **HIGH — statuses API not scoped by workspace** | `GET /statuses/` lists every workspace's statuses; any user can update/delete another workspace's status by id; `StatusCreate` has no `workspace_id` so create likely 500s | All status reads/writes scoped to the caller's workspace (404 for other workspaces); create sets `workspace_id` from the current workspace | found in [BaseDAO spec Q4](../../tests/unit/dao/base.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-036 | `client-declared-size-trusted` | `app/managers/attachment_manager.py` (`confirm_upload`) | **HIGH — attachment size limit only checks the client-declared size** | 10 MB limit checked against `file_size` declared at sign time; actual Cloudinary `bytes` never checked on the normal confirm path (mobile path does check) | Confirm rejects (and cleans up) uploads whose actual Cloudinary `bytes` exceed `MAX_FILE_SIZE_BYTES` | found in [attachment_allowlist spec notes](../../tests/unit/utils/attachment_allowlist.spec.md) — verify whether the Cloudinary upload signature already caps size | open |
| BUG-037 | `internal-error-leak` | `app/api/v1/endpoints/auth.py` (login and possibly other auth endpoints) | Login returns internal exception text on unexpected errors | Non-`ValueError` exceptions → 500 with `detail=f"Login failed: {e!s}"` (DB errors etc. leak to clients) | Unexpected errors return a generic 500 message; details only in server logs | found in [security spec notes](../../tests/unit/core/security.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-038 | `timing-account-enumeration` | `app/dao/profile.py` (`authenticate`) | Login timing reveals which emails are registered | Unknown email returns immediately; known email runs bcrypt (~0.25 s) | Always run a bcrypt verify (against a dummy hash for unknown emails) so timing is the same | found in [security spec notes](../../tests/unit/core/security.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-039 | `middleware-order` | `app/main.py` (middleware registration) | Middleware order is the reverse of what the code intends; 500s lack CORS headers | Comment says "first added = outermost" but Starlette makes the LAST added outermost, so CORS is innermost and unhandled-exception 500s have no CORS/security headers | Register middleware so CORS is outermost (per the stated intent); 4xx/5xx responses carry CORS headers | found in [middleware / utc_json_middleware spec notes](../../tests/unit/core/middleware.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-040 | `secret-in-query-string` | `app/api/v1/endpoints/mobile_upload.py` | Mobile-upload token travels in the URL query string | Public upload token is a query parameter, so it lands in request logs and proxy/access logs | Token sent in a header or POST body (also covered by middleware redaction, see middleware spec) | found in [middleware spec notes](../../tests/unit/core/middleware.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-100 | `unordered-pagination` | `app/dao/base.py` (`get_by_workspace`, `get_multi`); also `app/dao/factory.py` (`DAOFactory.get_by_workspace` — a line-for-line override that shadows the base method, so the fix must be repeated there or the override deleted; [factory DAO spec HR-6 line](../../tests/unit/dao/factory.spec.md)); also `app/dao/machine_item.py` (`DAOMachineItem.get_by_workspace` — the same kind of copy; [machine item DAO spec HR-6 line](../../tests/unit/dao/machine_item.spec.md)); also `app/dao/production_line.py` and `app/dao/production_formula.py` (`get_by_workspace` — the same kind of copy; [production line DAO spec HR-6 line](../../tests/unit/dao/production_line.spec.md), [production formula DAO spec HR-6 line](../../tests/unit/dao/production_formula.spec.md)) | BaseDAO list methods have no sort order | No `ORDER BY`; paging with `skip`/`limit` can repeat or skip rows (affects every inherited list) Evidence (order workflow service verifier, 2026-11-09, `GET /order-workflows/` through `OrderWorkflowService.get_workflows`): workflows A and B; after updating A the list returns [B, A], and paging with `limit=1` never shows A. | Order by `id` ascending before `skip`/`limit` (stable paging); display/name order stays the manager's job (HR-6, narrowed by reviewer for BaseDAO) | [spec HR-6 violations](../../tests/unit/dao/base.spec.md) | open |

| BUG-120 | `hr-1-incomplete-trim` | `app/utils/attachment_allowlist.py` (`sanitize_file_name`) | Attachment file name can keep a leading/trailing space | Whitespace stripped once, then dots once: `". report.pdf"` → `" report.pdf"`, `"report.pdf ."` → `"report.pdf "` | HR-1: leading/trailing whitespace and dots removed repeatedly until none remain | [spec HR-1 line](../../tests/unit/utils/attachment_allowlist.spec.md) | open |

| BUG-122 | `unicode-control-chars-kept` | `app/utils/attachment_allowlist.py` (`sanitize_file_name`) | Invisible Unicode control/format characters (bidi override, zero-width, C1) kept in file names | Only ASCII controls and `<>:"\|?*` removed; `invoice‮fdp.txt` displays as `invoicetxt.pdf` | Remove every Unicode `Cc`/`Cf` character before trimming and the empty check | [spec Q2](../../tests/unit/utils/attachment_allowlist.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-123 | `unnormalised-media-type-compare` | `app/utils/attachment_allowlist.py` (`normalize_upload_request`) | MIME type check is case-sensitive and rejects parameters | Exact match: `Image/PNG`, `text/csv; charset=utf-8` rejected (400); stored MIME kept as sent | Use text before first `;`, trimmed and lowercased, for the check; return/store the normalised form; error message quotes the value as sent | [spec Q3](../../tests/unit/utils/attachment_allowlist.spec.md) | open |

| BUG-140 | `flag-unreachable-from-api` | `app/utils/audit_logger.py` (fix in account/invoice-payment endpoints, services, `AccountManager`, `InvoicePaymentManager`) | Financial audit rows never record IP address or user agent | `log_financial_audit` accepts `ip_address`/`user_agent`, but no caller passes them; every API-written row has both `NULL` | Request's client IP (as resolved by trusted-proxy handling, never raw `X-Forwarded-For`) and user agent are passed from endpoint → service → manager into every `log_financial_audit` call (per BUG-010 precedent) | [spec Q1](../../tests/unit/utils/audit_logger.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-141 | `money-as-float` | `app/utils/audit_logger.py` (`extract_relevant_fields`) | Money amounts stored in the audit JSON as floats | `Decimal` converted to `float`: `2000.00` → `2000.0`, digits beyond a double's precision lost | `Decimal` stored as an exact fixed-point string (`"2000.00"`, never exponent notation); non-finite → `"NaN"`/`"Infinity"`/`"-Infinity"` | [spec Q2](../../tests/unit/utils/audit_logger.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-142 | `unserialisable-json-payload` | `app/utils/audit_logger.py` (`log_financial_audit`, `extract_relevant_fields`) | Non-JSON value in audit `changes` fails the business operation | Only top-level `Decimal`/`datetime`/`date` converted in snapshots, nothing in hand-built `changes`; `UUID`/`time`/`timedelta`/set/nested `Decimal` → `StatementError` at flush, caller's operation fails (500) | All `changes` values made JSON-safe recursively (Decimal/datetime/date/time/UUID/enum/non-finite float converted, non-str keys → str, tuple/set → list, `str()` fallback); caller's dict not mutated; audit write never fails on content | [spec Q3](../../tests/unit/utils/audit_logger.spec.md) | open |

| BUG-160 | `shape-based-value-rewrite` | `app/utils/datetime_serialize.py` (fix in `app/schemas/base.py`, `app/core/utc_json_middleware.py`) | Free-text strings shaped like a naive date-time get a `Z` appended in API responses | `ensure_utc_z_in_json` rewrites any string of the shape `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SS[.f]` regardless of field type (incl. impossible values like `9999-99-99T99:99:99`), via `AppResponseModel` and the `/api/` middleware | Only `datetime` fields gain the UTC `Z` (type-level serializer, not string pattern matching); `str` fields returned exactly as stored; until then the walker never rewrites a string that is not a real calendar date-time | [spec Q1](../../tests/unit/utils/datetime_serialize.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-161 | `regex-dollar-trailing-newline` | `app/utils/datetime_serialize.py` | Date-time followed by a trailing newline gets `Z` after the newline | Pattern anchored with `$`, which matches before one trailing `\n`; `"2026-01-01T10:00:00\n"` becomes `"2026-01-01T10:00:00\nZ"` | Match the whole string only (`\Z` / `fullmatch`); a trailing newline leaves the string unchanged | [spec Q2](../../tests/unit/utils/datetime_serialize.spec.md) | open |

| BUG-650 | `null-treated-as-required` | `app/managers/ledger_manager.py` (`reconcile_machine_item`, `reconcile_inventory`) | Reconcile crashes when the latest ledger entry has no average price | Latest entry exists with empty `avg_price_after` → `TypeError` (500), nothing corrected | Empty `avg_price_after` on the latest entry treated as `Decimal("0.00")`, same as no entry; reconcile completes | [spec Q7](../../tests/unit/managers/ledger_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-651 | `ledger-chain-broken-by-adjustment` | `app/managers/ledger_manager.py` (`reconcile_machine_item`, `reconcile_inventory`) | The reconciliation ledger row breaks the ledger's running balance chain | Adjustment row written with `qty_before` = the snapshot's old quantity and `quantity` = the difference, while the previous ledger row already ended at the ledger quantity — the chain shows a movement that never happened | Adjustment row keeps the chain continuous: `quantity = 0`, `qty_before = qty_after` = the ledger quantity (values/prices unchanged accordingly); the snapshot's old quantity and the discrepancy recorded in `notes` only; inventory notes additionally end with `. Avg price: snapshot was <old>, ledger shows <new>` (best-practice default, delegated by reviewer) | [spec Q8](../../tests/unit/managers/ledger_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-652 | `audit-stamp-missing-on-change` | `app/managers/ledger_manager.py` (`reconcile_inventory`) | Inventory reconcile does not record who changed the stock row | Reconcile changes `qty` (and `avg_price`) but leaves `updated_by` as it was | `updated_by` = the reconciling user whenever reconcile changes the snapshot (HR-9) | [spec Q9](../../tests/unit/managers/ledger_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-653 | `incomplete-balance-comparison` | `app/managers/ledger_manager.py` (`reconcile_inventory`) | Inventory reconcile ignores a wrong average price when quantities match | Quantity equal but average price different → `status: "balanced"`, price left wrong | An average-price difference (quantities equal) is also corrected: snapshot `avg_price` set to the ledger's latest average price, reported as `adjusted` | [spec Q10](../../tests/unit/managers/ledger_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-654 | `signed-total-summed-unsigned` | `app/managers/ledger_manager.py` (`calculate_project_component_total_cost`) | Project component total cost adds outgoing movements instead of subtracting them | Total is the plain sum of `total_cost` over all rows of the component, whatever the transaction type | Outgoing types (`transfer_out`, `damaged`) are subtracted; the total is the net cost consumed by the component | [spec Q11](../../tests/unit/managers/ledger_manager.spec.md) | open — revisit after launch (owner 2026-11-10; projects skipped until after launch, test stays) |
| BUG-655 | `wrong-column-copied-from-sibling` | `app/managers/ledger_manager.py` (fix in `app/dao/inventory_ledger.py` `InventoryLedgerDAO.calculate_balance`) | Inventory balance and inventory reconcile crash whenever the bucket has history | `calculate_balance` was copied from the machine ledger DAO and still reads `value_after`, a column the inventory ledger does not have; any bucket with ≥ 1 ledger entry raises `AttributeError` (500) | Balance = latest entry's `(qty_after, qty_after × avg_price_after)`, empty `avg_price_after` → value `Decimal("0.00")` | [spec Q12](../../tests/unit/managers/ledger_manager.spec.md) | open |

| BUG-661 | `hr-10-unlocked-state-transition` | `app/managers/inventory_movements.py` (`_machine_out`, `_machine_in`) | Machine stock posting does not lock the machine-item row | The `machine_items` row is read without a row lock; concurrent postings to the same machine/item lose updates and can over-draw machine stock | HR-10: the machine-item row is read with `SELECT … FOR UPDATE`; the loser sees the new quantity and gets the normal 400 "Insufficient machine stock" when short. | BUG-663 | `hr-4-soft-deleted-location-accepted` | `app/managers/inventory_movements.py` (`_machine_out`, `_machine_in`) | Soft-deleted machine accepted as a stock posting location | A soft-deleted machine is accepted as a stock location | HR-4: a soft-deleted machine is treated as not found → 404 `Machine with ID {id} not found` | [spec HR-4 violation](inventory_movements.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-664 | `ambiguous-latest-row-tiebreak` | `app/managers/inventory_movements.py` (fix in `InventoryLedgerDAO.get_latest_entry` / `MachineItemLedgerDAO.get_latest_entry`) | "Latest ledger entry" picked by timestamp alone can pick a stale row | Several ledger rows for the same bucket written in one save can share the identical `performed_at`; the DB returns any one of them as "latest", so stock/avg cost can be silently rolled back to an older row's values before the next movement is applied | "Latest" ordered by `performed_at` descending, then `id` descending, so the most recently inserted row always wins even when timestamps tie | [spec Q1](inventory_movements.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-665 | `unvalidated-quantity-sign` | `app/managers/inventory_movements.py` (`post_stock_out`, `post_stock_in`) | Zero or negative quantity accepted and reverses the movement direction | `qty` is not validated; a stock-out with `qty = -5` passes the "enough stock" check, *adds* to stock and writes a ledger row with a negative quantity labelled as an outgoing transaction; `qty = 0` writes a zero-quantity movement | `qty < 1` rejected with 400 `Quantity must be at least 1, got {qty}` before anything is written (exact wording: best-practice default, delegated by reviewer) | [spec Q2](inventory_movements.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-666 | `unchecked-foreign-key-workspace-scope` | `app/managers/inventory_movements.py` (`_storage_out`, `_storage_in`, `_machine_out`, `_machine_in`) | Item posted to stock is never checked to belong to the workspace | The item id is not looked up at all: an item from another workspace is accepted into this workspace's stock/ledger/activity rows; a non-existent item id fails with a raw database `IntegrityError` instead of a clean 404 | An item not found in `workspace_id` (non-existent, or belongs to another workspace) is rejected with 404 `Item with ID {id} not found` before anything is written | [spec Q3](inventory_movements.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-680 | `python-only-uniqueness` | `app/managers/po_receive_inventory.py` | Post/reversal idempotency (one ledger row per receive line / PO line source) not enforced by the database | The "already posted" / "already reversed" checks are a Python `SELECT` before `INSERT` with no unique constraint backing them; two concurrent calls for the same receive line or PO line (e.g. voiding the same PO twice at once) both pass the check and both write a ledger row, double-moving stock | Partial unique index on `(workspace_id, source_type, source_id)` on `inventory_ledger` and `machine_item_ledger`, for the `po_receive_event_item`, `po_receive_event_item_reversal` and `purchase_order_reversal` source types; a concurrent duplicate raises an integrity violation, surfaced as 409, never a second stock movement (per BUG-003 precedent) | [spec P1](../../tests/unit/managers/po_receive_inventory.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-681 | `unvalidated-code-input` | `app/managers/po_receive_inventory.py` (fix in `app/schemas/purchase_order.py`) | PO `destination_type` is unrestricted free text; any other spelling silently skips inventory posting | `destination_type` accepts any string; `_location_for_po` compares it exactly to `'storage'`/`'machine'`, so `'Storage'`, `'storage '`, `'factory'` etc. fall through as "no location" — receiving records the quantities on the PO but posts no stock and logs no error | `destination_type` trimmed and lower-cased by the PO create/update schemas; only `storage`, `machine`, `project` accepted, anything else 422; existing bad rows need a data clean-up (per BUG-022 precedent) | [spec P2](../../tests/unit/managers/po_receive_inventory.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-682 | `reversal-ignores-dependent-records` | `app/managers/po_receive_inventory.py` | Voiding a PO removes units already sent back in a purchase return a second time | `reverse_po_receive_inventory` reverses every posted receive line in full; completed purchase returns on the same PO are not considered, so units already returned to the supplier are deducted from stock again by the void | The void nets out completed returns per PO line: the total quantity removed for a PO line equals received minus already-returned quantity, so the destination ends where it was before the PO, not below it (reviewer accepted suggestion; netting bookkeeping — best-practice default, delegated by reviewer) | [spec Q1](../../tests/unit/managers/po_receive_inventory.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-683 | `reversal-uses-live-state-not-recorded` | `app/managers/po_receive_inventory.py` | Void reverses stock at the PO's current destination, not where the original posting actually went | Reversal quantity/direction come from the receive line's delta, but the location used is the PO's destination *at void time*; only "some" original posting is checked to exist in either ledger, not that it was at the same place — if the destination changed after receiving, the wrong location is debited/credited and the original one is never restored | Each reversal is made at the location recorded on the original ledger row (factory + inventory type, or machine), whatever the PO's destination is at void time — including when it is now a project or other non-stock destination (reviewer accepted suggestion; project case — best-practice default, delegated by reviewer) | [spec Q3](../../tests/unit/managers/po_receive_inventory.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-684 | `write-to-soft-deleted-related-row` | `app/managers/po_receive_inventory.py` | Receiving posts stock into a soft-deleted factory or machine | Destination lookup filters only by workspace id, not `is_deleted`; receiving against a PO whose destination was soft-deleted after PO creation succeeds and adds stock to the deleted location, invisible in inventory screens | Receiving (not void reversals) into a soft-deleted factory or machine is rejected with 400 before anything is written; message decided by best practice — `"Factory with ID {id} has been deleted"` / `"Machine with ID {id} has been deleted"` (reviewer accepted suggestion; message — best-practice default, delegated by reviewer) | [spec Q4](../../tests/unit/managers/po_receive_inventory.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-700 | `get-returns-soft-deleted` | `app/managers/inventory_manager.py` | `get_inventory` returns soft-deleted inventory rows | A soft-deleted inventory row is returned by id with no error | Deleted → 404 by default; new `include_deleted=True` parameter returns it | [HR-4 violation](../../tests/unit/managers/inventory_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-701 | `unordered-pagination` | `app/managers/inventory_manager.py` | Inventory list has no stable sort order | `get_inventory_page` orders by item name case-sensitively (database collation), then `id` | Item name case-insensitive ascending, then inventory `id` ascending, before `skip`/`limit` | [HR-6 violation](../../tests/unit/managers/inventory_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-702 | `unescaped-like-wildcards` | `app/managers/inventory_manager.py` | Inventory/stats search text acts as SQL wildcards | Search text placed in `ILIKE '%…%'` unescaped; `%`/`_` alone match every row | `%`, `_` and `\` match literally in item name and unit search | [HR-8 violation](../../tests/unit/managers/inventory_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-703 | `audit-stamp-on-noop` | `app/managers/inventory_manager.py` | `update_inventory` stamps `updated_by` on no-op updates | `updated_by` written on every successful update, including no-op ones | `updated_by` written only when a field actually changes value | [HR-9 violation](../../tests/unit/managers/inventory_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-704 | `hr-10-unlocked-state-transition` | `app/managers/inventory_manager.py` | `update_inventory` reads stock quantity without a row lock | Two concurrent adjustments both use the same "before" quantity | Row read `FOR UPDATE OF inventory` (the load joins `items` through a joined eager load, so a plain `FOR UPDATE` is refused — BUG-029 rule); the second request sees the first one's committed quantity | [HR-10 violation](../../tests/unit/managers/inventory_manager.spec.md) · [service R1a](../../tests/unit/services/inventory_service.spec.md) | open |

| BUG-706 | `unordered-pagination` | `app/managers/inventory_manager.py` (the missing `ORDER BY` is in `app/dao/factory.py` `get_active_factories` — fix there; [factory DAO spec HR-6 line](../../tests/unit/dao/factory.spec.md)) | Incoming summary processes factories with no sort order | `get_incoming_summary` (no `factory_id`) processes factories in whatever order the database returns | Factories processed in name case-insensitive ascending order, then `id` ascending | [HR-6 violation](../../tests/unit/managers/inventory_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-707 | `falsy-zero-treated-as-missing` | `app/managers/inventory_manager.py` | Zero average price records ledger `total_cost` as empty | Ledger `total_cost` only computed when `avg_price` is "truthy"; `avg_price = 0` gives `total_cost = NULL` | `total_cost` computed whenever `unit_cost` is not `None`; `0` gives `total_cost = 0.00` | [spec P1](../../tests/unit/managers/inventory_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-708 | `unbounded-decimal-input` | `app/managers/inventory_manager.py` (fix in `app/schemas/inventory.py`) | `avg_price` with too many decimals/digits accepted | `10.005` stored rounded while ledger `total_cost` computed unrounded; ≥10^13 fails at flush with a database error | `InventoryCreate`/`InventoryUpdate` reject `avg_price` with more than 2 decimals or more than 13 integer digits (422) before anything is written | [spec P2](../../tests/unit/managers/inventory_manager.spec.md) | open |

| BUG-710 | `unvalidated-foreign-key-reference` | `app/managers/inventory_manager.py` | Inventory can be created for a catalog item of another workspace | `item_id` is never checked against the caller's workspace; an item of another workspace is accepted and exposes its name/unit | The item must exist in the caller's workspace; otherwise 404 `Item with ID {item_id} not found`, nothing written | [spec Q1](../../tests/unit/managers/inventory_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-711 | `unvalidated-numeric-range` | `app/managers/inventory_manager.py` (fix in `app/schemas/inventory.py`) | Negative `qty` and `avg_price` accepted | No minimum enforced anywhere; negative quantity/price stored and surfaced in KPIs with no explanatory ledger entry | `InventoryCreate`/`InventoryUpdate` reject `qty < 0` and `avg_price < 0` with 422; nothing written | [spec Q2](../../tests/unit/managers/inventory_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-712 | `unlogged-value-change` | `app/managers/inventory_manager.py` | Average price can change with no ledger entry | A ledger entry is written only when quantity changes (update) or qty > 0 (create); an `avg_price`-only change has no ledger record and is silently reverted by the next ledger-driven sync | Any change to `avg_price` writes a ledger `inventory_adjustment` entry (quantity 0 when qty unchanged, before/after prices set) so the ledger stays the source of truth | [spec Q3](../../tests/unit/managers/inventory_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-713 | `null-value-treated-as-absent` | `app/managers/inventory_manager.py` | Update cannot clear `note` or `avg_price` | Every field sent as `null` is ignored (`exclude_none`); `note`/`avg_price` can never be cleared through the API | Explicit `null` clears `note` and `avg_price`; `qty: null` is rejected with 422 (`qty` cannot be empty) | [spec Q4](../../tests/unit/managers/inventory_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-714 | `soft-deleted-conflict-not-restored` | `app/managers/inventory_manager.py` | Creating over a soft-deleted row fails with an opaque database conflict | Pre-check only looks at live rows; insert then raises a unique-constraint `IntegrityError`, surfaced as a generic 409 with no visible existing row | The soft-deleted row is restored and updated with the submitted qty/avg_price/note (with the initial ledger entry as for a new row), returned as the created record | [spec Q5](../../tests/unit/managers/inventory_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-715 | `unvalidated-foreign-key-reference` | `app/managers/inventory_manager.py` | Inventory can be created in a soft-deleted factory | The factory check on create finds soft-deleted factories too | A soft-deleted factory is treated as not found: 404 `Factory with ID {factory_id} not found`, nothing written | [spec Q6](../../tests/unit/managers/inventory_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-716 | `off-by-boundary-noop-check` | `app/managers/inventory_manager.py` | "Clear stock" leaves a negative quantity untouched | `qty <= 0` is treated as the no-op case, so a negative quantity (e.g. from an over-issue) is never brought back to zero | A negative quantity is also set to 0 with a "Stock cleared" ledger entry; only `qty` exactly 0 is a no-op | [spec Q7](../../tests/unit/managers/inventory_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-717 | `voided-record-not-excluded` | `app/managers/inventory_manager.py` | Voided purchase orders still count as incoming stock | The incoming summary only excludes orders in their workflow's terminal status; a voided PO's unreceived quantity is reported indefinitely | Voided purchase orders are excluded from the incoming summary | [spec Q8](../../tests/unit/managers/inventory_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-718 | `get-returns-soft-deleted` | `app/managers/inventory_manager.py` | Per-factory incoming summary accepts a soft-deleted factory | `factory_id` of a soft-deleted factory is summarised normally, while the workspace-wide summary leaves that same factory out | A soft-deleted factory is treated as not found (404); an inactive-but-live factory is still summarised | [spec Q9](../../tests/unit/managers/inventory_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-719 | `unordered-pagination` | `app/managers/inventory_manager.py` | Per-type breakdown in inventory stats has no fixed order | `by_type` rows come back in whatever order the database groups them, which can change between calls | `by_type` ordered by inventory type in enum order: STORAGE, DAMAGED, WASTE, SCRAP | [spec Q10](../../tests/unit/managers/inventory_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-720 | `hr-10-unlocked-state-transition` | `app/managers/sales_manager.py` | State transitions read without a row lock (HR-10), across 7 methods | `complete_delivery`, `cancel_delivery`, `create_delivery_with_items`, `fulfill_service_item`, `mark_order_complete`, `set_section_confirm` and `set_approval` all check a row's state and then change it without `SELECT … FOR UPDATE`; concurrent requests can both deduct stock, both cancel/complete, together exceed planned capacity, both log a duplicate event, or race an unconfirm/invoice finalization | Each method's relevant row(s) (delivery, order, order line, or approver+order) are read `FOR UPDATE` before the check; the loser of a race sees the new state and gets the method's normal wrong-state error. One bug bundling all seven occurrences of the same root cause, per BUG-035's precedent of bundling same-root-cause symptoms under one ID | [spec HR-10 violations](../../../tests/unit/managers/sales_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-721 | `unchecked-status-before-transition` | `app/managers/sales_manager.py` (`complete_delivery`) | `complete_delivery` accepts a delivery in any status | The delivery's current status is not checked at all: `planned`, `delivered` and `cancelled` deliveries are all completed the same way — a `delivered` delivery can be completed again (double-deducting stock and delivered quantity), a `cancelled` one still deducts stock | Only a `planned` delivery can be completed; otherwise `ValueError("Only planned deliveries can be completed (this delivery is '<status>')")` (400 at the API), nothing changed | [spec Q1](../../../tests/unit/managers/sales_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-722 | `client-controlled-status-on-create` | `app/managers/sales_manager.py` (fix in `app/schemas/sales_delivery.py`, `SalesDeliveryCreate`) | A delivery can be created directly in any status | `SalesDeliveryCreate.delivery_status` defaults to `'planned'` but accepts any string and is stored as sent; a delivery created as `'delivered'`, `'cancelled'` or a made-up value reserves no capacity and (BUG-721) can still be completed | A new delivery always starts as `planned`; `delivery_status` is removed from the create schema (or ignored) | [spec Q2](../../../tests/unit/managers/sales_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-723 | `missing-cross-entity-ownership-check` | `app/managers/sales_manager.py` (`create_delivery_with_items`) | A delivery can include an order line from a different sales order | Each requested `sales_order_item_id` is looked up only by workspace, not by the delivery's own `sales_order_id`; a line from another order of the same workspace is accepted, and completing the delivery credits that other order while deducting stock at this order's factory, booked to this order's customer | A line that does not belong to the delivery's sales order is rejected with `ValueError("Sales order item <id> does not belong to sales order <order id>")` (400), nothing written | [spec Q3](../../../tests/unit/managers/sales_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-724 | `same-request-duplicates-bypass-check` | `app/managers/sales_manager.py` (`create_delivery_with_items`) | Repeating an order line in one delivery request bypasses the capacity check | The "available to plan" capacity is computed from already-flushed delivery lines only; two entries for the same order line in one request are each checked against the same pre-request capacity, so together they can exceed it | The same order line may appear only once per delivery request; a repeat is rejected with `ValueError` (400), nothing written | [spec Q4](../../../tests/unit/managers/sales_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-725 | `late-validation-blocks-completion` | `app/managers/sales_manager.py` (`create_delivery_with_items`) | Fractional quantity accepted when planning a catalog delivery line | Planning 2.5 units of a catalog (stock-tracked) line succeeds; `complete_delivery`'s whole-units check then always rejects it, so the delivery can never be completed and must be cancelled and re-planned | Planning rejects a fractional quantity on a catalog (`item_id` set) line with the same 400 "fractional quantity" message, before anything is written; free-text lines may stay fractional | [spec Q5](../../../tests/unit/managers/sales_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-726 | `unscoped-uniqueness-constraint` | `app/managers/sales_manager.py` (+ DB migration for the unique index) | Order/delivery numbers unique across all workspaces instead of per workspace | The unique index on `sales_order_number` / `delivery_number` is global; workspace B's first `SO-2026-001` collides with workspace A's existing `SO-2026-001` and fails at flush, even though nothing is wrong in workspace B | Numbers are unique per workspace (unique index on `(workspace_id, sales_order_number)` / `(workspace_id, delivery_number)` instead of the global one); every workspace starts at `-001` | [spec Q6](../../../tests/unit/managers/sales_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-727 | `count-then-insert-race` | `app/managers/sales_manager.py` (+ `app/dao/sales_order.py`, `app/dao/sales_delivery.py`) | Order/delivery numbering is not race-free | The next number is computed by counting existing rows with no lock or sequence; two concurrent creates in the same workspace can compute the same `<n>` and one fails at flush with `IntegrityError` | Number generation is race-free (e.g. a per-workspace, per-year counter row read `FOR UPDATE`, or a retry on unique violation); concurrent creates both succeed with distinct numbers | [spec Q7](../../../tests/unit/managers/sales_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-728 | `missing-workspace-scope` | `app/managers/sales_manager.py` (`create_sales_order_with_items`) | Sales order accepts another workspace's (or soft-deleted) customer, factory or catalog items | `account_id`, `factory_id` and line `item_id`s are stored as sent; only "does the row exist at all" is enforced by the DB, so ids from another workspace or soft-deleted rows are accepted, and a non-existent id raises `IntegrityError` instead of a clean 404 | Customer, factory and every catalog item must be live and belong to the caller's workspace; otherwise the create is rejected (404 "… not found") before anything is written | [spec Q8](../../../tests/unit/managers/sales_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-729 | `docstring-constraint-not-enforced` | `app/managers/sales_manager.py` (`create_sales_order_with_items`) | Sales order customer is not required to have the `client` tag | The model documents the customer as "must have 'client' tag", but nothing in the backend checks it; any account, e.g. a supplier-only one, can be the customer | Creating a sales order with an account that lacks the `client` tag is rejected with 400 "Selected account is not a client" | [spec Q9](../../../tests/unit/managers/sales_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-730 | `missing-workspace-scope` | `app/managers/sales_manager.py` (`create_delivery_with_items`, `update_delivery`) | Delivery method not checked for workspace or soft-deletion | `delivery_method_id` is stored as sent on create and edit, with no workspace or deleted-row check; a delivery can show another workspace's method name | The delivery method must be live and in the caller's workspace; otherwise `ValueError("Delivery method <id> not found")` (400), nothing written | [spec Q10](../../../tests/unit/managers/sales_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-731 | `refunded-quantity-ignored` | `app/managers/sales_manager.py` (`_recompute_is_fully_delivered`, `fulfill_service_item`) | Refunded quantity ignored by `is_fully_delivered` recompute and by re-fulfilling a service line | `_recompute_is_fully_delivered` requires `delivered >= ordered` on every line, ignoring `quantity_refunded` — inconsistent with the completion helpers, which do count it — so after a refund the order can be "complete-able" but never "fully delivered". `fulfill_service_item` re-fulfilling a line sets `delivered = ordered`, also ignoring `quantity_refunded`, so a line refunded after being fulfilled gets over-counted (`delivered + refunded > ordered`) when fulfilled again | Both use the same done-test as `all_deliverable_items_delivered` / `all_fulfilment_items_fulfilled`: a line counts as done when `delivered + refunded >= ordered`. Re-fulfilling sets `delivered = ordered − refunded` and is a no-op when already done | [spec Q11, Q12](../../../tests/unit/managers/sales_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-732 | `exclude-none-blocks-clearing` | `app/managers/sales_manager.py` (`update_delivery`; fix also in `app/schemas/sales_delivery.py`, `SalesDeliveryEditRequest`) | Editing a planned delivery cannot clear tracking number, notes or scheduled date | The update uses `exclude_none`, so a field sent as explicit `null` is treated like an omitted one and left unchanged — once set, `scheduled_date`/`tracking_number`/`notes` can only be replaced, never cleared | A field explicitly sent as `null` clears it (`scheduled_date`, `tracking_number`, `notes`); `delivery_method_id` stays required and a `null` for it is rejected with 422; omitted fields stay unchanged | [spec Q13](../../../tests/unit/managers/sales_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-733 | `hr-9-stamp-never-written` | `app/managers/sales_manager.py` (`complete_delivery`, `cancel_delivery`, `update_delivery`) | Delivery edit/cancel/complete never set `updated_by` | None of the three delivery-mutating methods write `updated_by`; it stays empty (only `updated_at` moves) however a delivery changed | `updated_by` is set to the acting user whenever edit/cancel/complete changes at least one field (HR-9); a no-op edit leaves it alone | [spec Q14](../../../tests/unit/managers/sales_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-734 | `untrimmed-optional-free-text` | `app/managers/sales_manager.py` (`complete_delivery`, `fulfill_service_item`) | Completion code not trimmed; blank handling differs between delivery and fulfilment | `complete_delivery` stores `""` as `""` and leaves a stored code unchanged on `None`; `fulfill_service_item` drops `None`/`""` but stores a whitespace-only code as typed; both show untrimmed whitespace in the activity log (`"(code:  )"`) | The code is trimmed in both methods; empty after trimming means "no code" (stored as `None`, not shown in the description) in both places | [spec Q15](../../../tests/unit/managers/sales_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-735 | `duplicated-prefix-in-formatted-string` | `app/managers/sales_manager.py` (`complete_delivery`) | Stock ledger note shows the order number as "SO-SO-…" | The ledger-note template adds its own `SO-` prefix in front of an order number that already starts with `SO-`, producing `"Delivery DEL-2026-004 for SO-SO-2026-010"` | Note reads `"Delivery DEL-2026-004 for SO-2026-010"` — order number used as stored, no extra prefix | [spec Q16](../../../tests/unit/managers/sales_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-736 | `vacuous-truth-on-empty-set` | `app/managers/sales_manager.py` (`approval_summary`) | Order with no approvers counts as fully approved | With `required_approvals` null and zero assigned approvers, `required` computes to `0`, so `approved_count (0) >= required (0)` is met — an order can be finalized with nobody having approved it | At least one approval is always required: with no approvers assigned, `required = max(1, number of assigned approvers)`, so approval with zero approvers is never met | [spec Q18](../../../tests/unit/managers/sales_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-737 | `inconsistent-sibling-guard` | `app/managers/sales_manager.py` (`add_approver`, `remove_approver`) | Adding/removing approvers not blocked after order completion or invoice lock, unlike withdrawing | `set_approval` blocks withdrawing an approval once the order is complete or its invoice is confirmed, but `add_approver`/`remove_approver` apply no such check — an approver (including one who already approved) can still be added or removed from a finalized/completed order | `add_approver`/`remove_approver` are rejected with 400 ("Cannot change approvers — order is complete" / "Cannot change approvers — invoice is locked") under the same checks `set_approval` already applies to withdrawing | [spec Q19](../../../tests/unit/managers/sales_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-740 | `audit-stamp-on-noop` | `app/managers/expense_order_manager.py` | `updated_by` / approval-reset / update-event fire on no-op edits | `updated_by` is written on every successful `update_expense_order` call, including an empty payload, all-`null` payload, or unchanged values (even on a completed order); `update_item` logs an `item_updated` event and resets approvals on every call, including an empty payload | `updated_by`, the approvals reset, and the `item_updated` event are all gated on at least one field actually changing value | [spec HR-9 line, Q14](../../../tests/unit/managers/expense_order_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-741 | `hr-10-unlocked-state-transition` | `app/managers/expense_order_manager.py` | `update_expense_order` edits a money record without a row lock | Checks "not completed" then edits without `FOR UPDATE`; a concurrent completion can land between check and write | Order row read `FOR UPDATE` before the completed/voided check; the loser sees the new state and gets the normal 400 | [spec HR-10 line](../../../tests/unit/managers/expense_order_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-742 | `hr-10-unlocked-state-transition` | `app/managers/expense_order_manager.py` | `delete_expense_order` deletes without a row lock and leaves a linked invoice behind | Checks "not completed" then hard-deletes without `FOR UPDATE` (a concurrent completion can be deleted with its order); delete succeeds even when the order has a linked draft/confirmed invoice, which then points to a non-existent order | Order row read `FOR UPDATE` before the completed check; delete refused with 400 "Void the expense order instead" when an invoice is linked | [spec HR-10 line, Q16](../../../tests/unit/managers/expense_order_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-743 | `hr-10-unlocked-state-transition` | `app/managers/expense_order_manager.py` | `add_item`/`update_item`/`remove_item` mutate lines and totals without a row lock | Item changes check "not completed" and recompute order totals from an unlocked read; concurrent edits can duplicate line numbers or lose a total update | Parent order row read `FOR UPDATE` before the check in all three methods; totals always equal the sum of the stored lines | [spec HR-10 line](../../../tests/unit/managers/expense_order_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-744 | `hr-10-unlocked-state-transition` | `app/managers/expense_order_manager.py` | `set_approval` changes an approval decision without a row lock | Checks readiness / "not completed" and writes the approval without locking the order | Order row read `FOR UPDATE` before the check, for both the approve and withdraw paths | [spec HR-10 line](../../../tests/unit/managers/expense_order_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-745 | `unescaped-like-wildcards` | `app/managers/expense_order_manager.py` | Hub search text is an unescaped `ILIKE` pattern | `search` is put into `ILIKE '%…%'` unescaped, so `%` or `_` alone matches every order (affects `list_expense_orders_for_hub` and everything built on it) | `%`, `_` and `\` in the search text match literally (same pattern as BUG-013) | [spec HR-8 line](../../../tests/unit/managers/expense_order_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-746 | `unordered-pagination` | `app/managers/expense_order_manager.py` | "Recent orders" widget sorts by the wrong key and has no tie-breaker | `list_expense_orders_recent_for_hub` orders by `updated_at` desc, `created_at` desc only (no `id` tie-breaker); since `updated_at` is `null` for a never-edited order and Postgres sorts `null` first descending, never-edited orders show before recently-edited ones | Ordered by `coalesce(updated_at, created_at)` desc, then `id` desc | [spec HR-6 line, Q23](../../../tests/unit/managers/expense_order_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-747 | `unordered-pagination` | `app/managers/expense_order_manager.py` | Dashboard "open by account" top-5 has unordered ties | `expense_order_financial_snapshot`'s `open_by_account` is a top-5 ordered only by `total_value`; equal totals are cut/ordered arbitrarily | Ties broken by account name case-insensitive ascending, then account id ascending | [spec HR-6 line](../../../tests/unit/managers/expense_order_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-748 | `unordered-pagination` | `app/managers/expense_order_manager.py` | Dashboard "unpaid pipeline" samples have unordered ties | `expense_order_financial_snapshot`'s unpaid-pipeline samples (top 3 per bucket) ordered only by outstanding amount; equal amounts give unstable samples | Ties broken by order `id` ascending | [spec HR-6 line](../../../tests/unit/managers/expense_order_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-749 | `unsafe-sequence-generation` | `app/managers/expense_order_manager.py` (+ migration) | Expense-number generation is globally unique, text-sorted, and race-prone | (1) A workspace-wide unique index (`ix_expense_orders_expense_number`) makes a second workspace's first order of the year collide with another workspace's number — permanent 409s; (2) the "highest" suffix is compared as text, so `…-999` sorts above `…-1000` and numbering breaks permanently after order 999; (3) no lock/sequence — two concurrent creates in one workspace can compute the same number, the loser's order is lost to a 409 | Uniqueness enforced per workspace only (drop the global index, keep `uq_eo_workspace_number`); next number = numeric max of the year's suffixes + 1, padded to at least 3 digits; number assignment is race-safe (locked per-workspace counter or retry on unique violation) | [spec Q6, Q7, Q8](../../../tests/unit/managers/expense_order_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-750 | `unvalidated-stored-reference` | `app/managers/expense_order_manager.py` (fix in `app/schemas/expense_order.py`) | `account_id` / `order_template_id` accepted without a workspace check | `account_id` on create/update, and `order_template_id` on a plain create, are stored with no check that they belong to this workspace or are live; a non-existent id fails at flush with `IntegrityError`, another workspace's id is stored and later disclosed (account name in search/dashboard; order claims to come from a template it cannot see) | `account_id` must be a live account of the same workspace (404 "Selected account not found" otherwise); `order_template_id` removed from `ExpenseOrderCreate` entirely — only `create_expense_order_from_template` sets it, from an already-validated template | [spec Q9, Q11](../../../tests/unit/managers/expense_order_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-751 | `client-writable-internal-field` | `app/managers/expense_order_manager.py` (fix in `app/schemas/expense_order.py`) | `invoice_id` and line `approved` are client-writable but system/internal-only | `ExpenseOrderUpdate.invoice_id` lets a client point an order at any invoice id (another order's, or another workspace's) with no check, not logged, doesn't reset approvals; `ExpenseOrderItemUpdate.approved` is stored but nothing in the backend reads a line's `approved` flag | `invoice_id` removed from `ExpenseOrderUpdate` — only the system links invoices; `approved` removed from `ExpenseOrderItemUpdate` | [spec Q10, Q22](../../../tests/unit/managers/expense_order_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-752 | `stale-cross-field-value` | `app/managers/expense_order_manager.py` | Category "other" keeps a stale cost-center id | `_validate_order_allocation` skips the cost-center check for category `other` but the caller never clears `cost_center_id`; an order re-categorised from `factory`/`department` to `other` (or created as `other` with a cost center) keeps the old id, and cost-center reports still count it | When the resulting category is `other`, `cost_center_id` is stored as `null` (create, update, and create-from-template) | [spec Q12](../../../tests/unit/managers/expense_order_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-753 | `null-update-ignored` | `app/managers/expense_order_manager.py` (fix in `app/schemas/expense_order.py`) | Explicit `null` cannot clear optional order/line fields | Update payloads use `exclude_unset` **and** drop `null` values before applying them, so sending `null` for `due_date`, `account_id`, `cost_center_id`, `description` (order) or `unit_price`, `unit`, `notes`, `description` (line) is silently ignored — the old value stays, response is 200 | An explicit `null` clears nullable fields (`exclude_unset` only, not `exclude_none`); required fields (`expense_category`, `expense_date`, `quantity`) reject `null` with 422 | [spec Q13](../../../tests/unit/managers/expense_order_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-754 | `missing-state-guard` | `app/managers/expense_order_manager.py` | Voided orders can still be edited, have lines/approvers changed, and be approved | The manager's mutation guards (`_guard_confirmed_updates`, `_guard_item_mutations`, and the checks in `add_approver`/`set_approval`) only check "completed", never "voided"; a voided order accepts detail edits, line add/edit/remove, new approvers, and approval (which can auto-create a fresh draft invoice for a voided order) | Voided orders are read-only for these paths: every mutation returns 400 "Expense order is voided" | [spec Q15](../../../tests/unit/managers/expense_order_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-755 | `vacuous-approval-met` | `app/managers/expense_order_manager.py` | Order with no approvers and no explicit requirement counts as fully approved | `approval_summary` sets `required = 0` when `required_approvals` is unset and the order has no approvers (e.g. after removing every approver), so "0 of 0 — met" is true and the next edit auto-creates a draft invoice with no one having approved | With no explicit `required_approvals` and no approvers, `required = 1` so approvals are not met; only an explicit `required_approvals = 0` waives approval | [spec Q17](../../../tests/unit/managers/expense_order_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-756 | `missing-state-guard` | `app/managers/expense_order_manager.py` | Approvers can be added or removed after the order is complete | `add_approver` and `remove_approver` have no completed-order check (unlike withdrawing an approval, which is blocked); on a completed order, approvers can be added or removed after the fact, changing what the finished order's approval count/audit trail shows | Adding or removing approvers on a completed order returns 400 "Expense order is complete" | [spec Q18](../../../tests/unit/managers/expense_order_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-757 | `non-idempotent-state-set` | `app/managers/expense_order_manager.py` | Approving/withdrawing a state the approver already has is recorded again | `set_approval` always writes and logs an event, even when the approver already has the requested `approved` value — double-clicking Approve moves `approved_at` to now and logs a second `approved` event; withdrawing when not approved logs a spurious `approval_withdrawn` event | Setting the state the approver already has is a no-op: no event logged, `approved_at` kept, row returned unchanged | [spec Q19](../../../tests/unit/managers/expense_order_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-758 | `incomplete-error-detail` | `app/managers/expense_order_manager.py` | "Not ready for approval" message omits which line is the problem | `approvability_gap_reason` covers every readiness check except line-level ones; when a line has no description or quantity 0, the approver only sees the generic "Order is not ready for approval" | The reason names the line problem: "Every expense line needs a description and a quantity above 0 before approvals can proceed" | [spec Q20](../../../tests/unit/managers/expense_order_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-759 | `hard-fail-on-optional-dependency` | `app/managers/expense_order_manager.py` | Creating from a template fails entirely when its default approver is no longer active | `create_expense_order_from_template` adds the template's `default_approver_id` via `add_approver`, which 400s if that user is no longer an active member; the whole create call fails and no order is created | The order is still created without that approver; an event records that the default approver was skipped (not an active member) | [spec Q21](../../../tests/unit/managers/expense_order_manager.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-800 | `hr-10-unlocked-state-transition` | `app/managers/po_return_inventory.py` (fix in `PurchaseOrderService.complete_return` and `app/managers/inventory_movements.py`) | Return completion posts stock without locking the return/stock row | `post_purchase_return_inventory`'s already-posted check and stock deduction run with no row lock and no unique constraint on the ledger source; two concurrent completions of the same return both pass the check and both deduct stock | HR-10: the completion path locks the row it decides on (the return row in `PurchaseOrderService.complete_return`, and/or the stock row in `inventory_movements`) so a concurrent second call sees the already-posted/completed state and deducts nothing | [spec — house-rule violations](../../tests/unit/managers/po_return_inventory.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-801 | `validation-missing-at-entry` | `app/managers/po_return_inventory.py` (fix belongs in the return-start code path — a different module, out of scope of this spec) | Fractional return quantity is accepted when a return starts, then permanently blocks its completion | For a storage/machine-destination PO, a return with a fractional `quantity_returned` (e.g. 1.5) can be started and its credit-note invoice confirmed; every later attempt to complete it fails with 400 (`_quantity_to_int` R2) and nothing changes — the only way out is to void the return | The return-start path rejects a fractional `quantity_returned` up front (400, nothing created) for storage/machine-destination POs; `post_purchase_return_inventory`'s completion-side check stays as a safety net, unchanged (reviewer accepted suggestion) | [spec Q1](../../tests/unit/managers/po_return_inventory.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-802 | `internal-id-in-user-message` | `app/managers/po_return_inventory.py` | Fractional-quantity return error names the internal PO-item id, not the line number the user sees | `_quantity_to_int`'s 400 detail is `Return line for PO item {po_item_id} has a fractional quantity; inventory requires whole units` — `po_item_id` is the PO line's internal database id | Message uses the PO line's `line_number` instead: `Return line for PO line {line_number} has a fractional quantity; inventory requires whole units`, matching `po_receive_inventory._delta_to_int`'s message shape (reviewer accepted suggestion) | [spec Q2](../../tests/unit/managers/po_return_inventory.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-803 | `ambiguous-latest-row-tiebreak` | `app/managers/po_return_inventory.py` (fix in `InventoryLedgerDAO.get_latest_entry`, same fix as BUG-664) | Return with 3+ lines of the same item can deduct from a stale stock snapshot | Before each storage deduction the stock row is re-synced from the "latest" storage-ledger row, chosen by `performed_at` alone; rows written earlier in the same completion share one `performed_at` (transaction start), so the third line may re-sync from the first line's row and deduct from the wrong quantity (e.g. stock 10, three returns of 3 → final stock 4 instead of 1) | "Latest" = `performed_at` descending, then `id` descending, so each line deducts from the stock left by the previous one (precedent BUG-664; best-practice default, delegated by reviewer) | [spec R15a](../../tests/unit/managers/po_return_inventory.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-820 | `hr-10-unlocked-state-transition` | `app/managers/to_inventory.py` (fix in `app/managers/inventory_movements.py`) | Transfer-order stock posting doesn't lock the source bucket/machine stock row | Source stock (factory bucket or machine stock) is checked for enough quantity and then reduced without a row lock; two concurrent postings (another transfer order, a work order consuming stock) can both pass the check and drive stock negative or lose one deduction | HR-10: the source stock row is read with `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` before the availability check (fix in `app/managers/inventory_movements.py`, `_storage_out` / `_machine_out`) | [spec HR-10 violation 1](./to_inventory.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-821 | `hr-10-unlocked-state-transition` | `app/managers/to_inventory.py` (fix in `app/managers/transfer_order_manager.py`) | Transfer order completion doesn't lock the order row, allowing duplicate posting | "Already posted?" is checked and then stock is moved without any lock or database uniqueness; two concurrent completions of the same transfer order both see "not posted" and both move the stock | HR-10: a line is posted at most once even under concurrent completion — `TransferOrderManager.mark_order_complete` locks the transfer order row with `SELECT … FOR UPDATE OF transfer_orders` before the completed/posted checks (the order read joins `statuses`, so a bare `FOR UPDATE` is refused — BUG-029 rule; noted from transfer_order_service fill-gaps G1, 2026-11-21) | [spec HR-10 violation 2](./to_inventory.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-822 | `unvalidated-stored-reference` | `app/managers/to_inventory.py` | Soft-deleted factory or machine accepted as transfer source/destination | When a transfer order is completed, stock is taken from / put into a factory or machine even if it has since been soft-deleted; only a missing id or one from another workspace is rejected (404) | A soft-deleted factory or machine is rejected exactly like a missing one (404 "Factory with ID {id} not found" / "Machine with ID {id} not found"), nothing written | [spec Q1](./to_inventory.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-823 | `unvalidated-code-input` | `app/managers/to_inventory.py` (fix in `app/schemas/transfer_order.py`) | Transfer order location type is free text; unknown/differently-cased types only fail at completion | An order with a source/destination type like `"Storage"`, `"warehouse"` or `""` can be created, filled with lines and approved; completing it fails with 400 "Unsupported source/destination location type: …", and the order can never be completed without editing it | `TransferOrderCreate`/`TransferOrderUpdate` accept only the known location types (source: `storage`, `damaged`, `machine`; destination: `storage`, `damaged`, `machine`, `project`) and reject anything else with 422 at entry; the completion-time check stays as a safety net | [spec Q2](./to_inventory.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-824 | `unbounded-decimal-input` | `app/managers/to_inventory.py` (fix in `app/schemas/transfer_order.py`) | Fractional, zero or negative transfer line quantities only rejected at completion | A line with quantity `2.5`, `0` or `-3` can be added and the order approved; completing it fails with 400 ("has a fractional quantity…" or "has no quantity to transfer"); approvals must then be withdrawn to fix the line | `TransferOrderItemCreate`/`TransferOrderItemUpdate` reject a quantity that is not a whole number greater than 0 with 422 at entry; the completion-time check stays as a safety net | [spec Q3](./to_inventory.spec.md) | open |
| BUG-825 | `dead-end-accepted-value` | `app/managers/to_inventory.py` (fix in `app/managers/transfer_order_manager.py`) | "Project" transfer destination can be chosen but can never be completed | An order with destination type `project` can be created, filled and approved; completion always fails with 400 "Project destination inventory posting is not yet supported", so the order is permanently stuck | While project posting is unsupported, creating or updating a transfer order with destination type `project` is rejected at entry (400 "Project destination is not yet supported"); an application-level check, not a 422 schema validator, since the intended status is 400 | [spec Q4](./to_inventory.spec.md) | open |

| BUG-948 | `delete-ignores-dependents` | `app/managers/item_manager.py` (`delete_item`; reached through `DELETE /items/{id}/`; 400 translation in `app/services/item_service.py`) | Item with stock or open orders can be deleted | `delete_item` never looks at stock or orders: an item with stock in a factory / on a machine or on an open purchase / sales / work / transfer order is soft-deleted (204); its stock and order lines stay | Refused, nothing written, while the item has an `inventory` row (`is_deleted = False`, any type) or a `machine_items` row with `qty ≠ 0`, or a line (`item_id`; work orders also `replaced_item_id`) on an open order: purchase `voided = False` and stage ≠ `Complete`; work `status` `DRAFT` / `IN_PROGRESS` (live order, live line); transfer `completed_at` NULL; sales `order_completed = False` (completed never blocks). Also while it has a `products` row (`is_deleted = False`) with `qty ≠ 0`, or a `production_batch_items` line on a batch with `status` `draft` / `in_progress` (`completed` / `cancelled` do not block) (owner's answer 2026-10-30, "Both block"). A `machine_items` row on a deleted machine does not block, because no list shows it and the user cannot clear it (owner's answer 2026-10-30, `machine_item_manager` Q5); nor does stock in a deleted factory: `inventory`, `products`, or `machine_items` on a machine of that factory, and the rows stay (owner's answer 2026-10-31). Deleted means `is_deleted`; `is_active` alone still blocks. A cross-workspace row follows the same state rule. A stock, order or batch row linking across workspaces is invalid data and creating one must be refused (owner's correction 2026-10-30; creation paths in other modules, logged by a separate audit); if such a row exists in a blocking state the delete is still refused, showing nothing of the other workspace. The check runs before any write, so the refusal wins over an unknown-user foreign-key error (Claude's reading of the owner's answer). The refusal lists **every** blocker: `ValueError("Item has stock or open orders", blockers)`, `blockers` = list of `{"type": "factory_stock" / "machine_stock" / "purchase_order" / "sales_order" / "work_order" / "transfer_order" / "product_stock" / "production_batch", "id": inventory row / machine_items row / order id / products row / batch id}`, one per row, order or batch, sorted by type in that order then id, then one generic `{"type": "other_workspace"}` with no id, last, once however many cross-workspace rows (Claude's recommendation; owner approved 2026-10-30, "yeah i agree with your recommendations"); 400 at the API with the list in the RFC 7807 `errors` member (`ValidationError(detail, errors=…)`) (owner's answers 2026-10-18, "Refuse while in use" and "show user … things that are stopping you"; as BUG-876; UI part BUG-949) | [spec Q2](../../tests/unit/managers/item_manager.spec.md) | open |

Reads the 400 body's `errors` list (spec FU-1 / FU-8); no backend test (owner 2026-10-18: "its a frontend thing but just make notes in the bugs") | [spec Q2](../../tests/unit/managers/item_manager.spec.md) | open |

| BUG-1040 | `python-rounding-differs-from-database` | `app/managers/inventory_movements.py` (`_machine_in` :320, `_machine_out` :247; also `_storage_out` :96, `_storage_in` :159, `weighted_avg_price` :37); `app/managers/product_manager.py` :218; `app/managers/work_order_manager.py` :762, :859, :860, :883; dead code on the removal checklist, same line: `app/managers/po_machine_inventory.py` :41, `app/managers/po_storage_inventory.py` :37 | Money rounded half to even in Python, half away from zero in the database | `quantize(Decimal('0.01'))` with no rounding argument uses the `decimal` default, half to even, while Postgres `Numeric(15, 2)` rounds half away from zero. `_machine_in` with `unit_cost` 0.125, qty 1 stores `unit_cost` 0.13, `total_cost` 0.12, `value_after` 0.13: one row disagrees with itself by 0.01 | All money rounds half away from zero everywhere (0.125 → 0.13, -1.005 → -1.01), matching the database (owner, 2026-10-30): every place listed passes `ROUND_HALF_UP` (in `decimal` that means away from zero), best through one shared helper. Tests and specs that pin half to even change with the fix: `inventory_movements.spec.md` R2a and `INVM-WAP-04`; `to_inventory.spec.md` R5 and `TOI-IN-08`, `TOI-IN-39`. **Stored rows (owner, 2026-10-31): "Restate them".** `total_cost`: no row expected, inputs are two-decimal and `qty` an integer; a row with `total_cost <> quantity * unit_cost` cannot be restated without guessing, the original price is stored nowhere. Averages and work order line unit costs: found exactly from each row's own columns, with `n = qty_before * avg_price_before + quantity * unit_cost`, `d = qty_before + quantity`, `qty_before > 0`, `quantity > 0`: `MOD(n * 200, d) = 0 AND MOD((n * 200) / d, 2) = 1 AND avg_price_after <> ROUND(n / d, 2)` (verified on both stock ledgers). Computable: that row's `avg_price_after` (always +0.01) and, on the machine ledger, `value_after`. Not computable without guessing: later entries, snapshots, transfers and work order costs that read the old average (BUG-712, BUG-993, BUG-640, BUG-664 break the chain). **Owner, 2026-10-31, "Found rows only":** only the found rows' own columns are corrected, later rows and current averages stay; the one approved in-place ledger edit. `scripts/restate_half_even_averages.py` also requires the stored value to be the half-even one, skips `inventory_ledger` rows with `source_type` `manual` / `adjustment`, reads `product_ledger` for `production` only, and leaves a found row that is the latest entry of its machine ledger or inventory ledger key, because the average in use is read from it ([spec Q2a](../../tests/unit/managers/machine_item_manager.spec.md)) | [spec FU-6](../../tests/unit/dao/machine_item_ledger.spec.md) | open |
